This verse thus reveals the deeper spiritual cost of the selective obedience and covenant-breaking described in verse 85.

Verse 87

وَلَقَدْ آتَيْنَا مُوسَى الْكِتَابَ وَقَفَّيْنَا مِن بَعْدِهِ بِالرُّسُلِ ۖ وَآتَيْنَا عِيسَى ابْنَ مَرْيَمَ الْبَيِّنَاتِ وَأَيَّدْنَاهُ بِرُوحِ الْقُدُسِ ۗ أَفَكُلَّمَا جَاءَكُمْ رَسُولٌ بِمَا لَا تَهْوَىٰ أَنفُسُكُمُ اسْتَكْبَرْتُمْ فَفَرِيقًا كَذَّبْتُمْ وَفَرِيقًا تَقْتُلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse begins by reminding the Children of Israel of the long line of divine guidance sent to them, starting with Prophet Moses, who received the Torah, and continuing with subsequent messengers, including Prophet Jesus, who was given clear signs and supported by the holy spirit (understood in Islam as the Angel Gabriel). It then directly challenges their historical reaction to these messengers. Despite receiving such continuous guidance, their response was often arrogance and rejection when the message did not align with their personal desires or expectations. This history included accusing some messengers of lying and even killing others. This verse sets the stage for discussing their reaction to the final messenger, Prophet Muhammad, in the verses that follow, by highlighting a recurring pattern of resistance to divine messages throughout their history.

Verse 88

وَقَالُوا قُلُوبُنَا غُلْفٌ ۚ بَل لَّعَنَهُمُ اللَّهُ بِكُفْرِهِمْ فَقَلِيلًا مَّا يُؤْمِنُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses a claim made by some among the Children of Israel when they were called to accept Prophet Muhammad and the message of the Qur'an. They responded by saying, "Our hearts are sealed," implying either sufficiency in their existing knowledge or an inability to comprehend the new message. The verse firmly refutes this claim, stating that it was not an inherent condition but a consequence of their own disbelief and rejection of the truth. Their hearts became unreceptive because they turned away first. As a result, Allah distanced them, and their faith is described as minimal. This verse highlights their initial reaction to the call of Islam and exposes the weakness of their justification for rejection, setting the stage for further rebuttals in the verses that follow.

Verse 89

وَلَمَّا جَاءَهُمْ كِتَابٌ مِّنْ عِندِ اللَّهِ مُصَدِّقٌ لِّمَا مَعَهُمْ وَكَانُوا مِن قَبْلُ يَسْتَفْتِحُونَ عَلَى الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا فَلَمَّا جَاءَهُم مَّا عَرَفُوا كَفَرُوا بِهِ ۚ فَلَعْنَةُ اللَّهِ عَلَى الْكَافِرِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse points out another inconsistency in the behavior of some among the Children of Israel regarding the advent of the Qur'an. They had previously anticipated the coming of a prophet and a book that would confirm their scriptures, and had even spoken about this with the pagan Arabs, expecting that this advent would bring them victory over their enemies. However, when the Qur'an came through Prophet Muhammad, fulfilling the description of a book confirming their own scriptures, they refused to believe in it, despite recognizing its truth. The verse condemns this denial of something they knew to be true and calls down Allah's curse upon those who actively disbelieve. This highlights their denial not just as a matter of ignorance, but as a conscious rejection of a known truth, linking their past anticipation to their current denial.

Verse 90

بِئْسَمَا اشْتَرَوْا بِهِ أَنفُسَهُمْ أَن يَكْفُرُوا بِمَا أَنزَلَ اللَّهُ بَغْيًا أَن يُنَزِّلَ اللَّهُ مِن فَضْلِهِ عَلَىٰ مَن يَشَاءُ مِنْ عِبَادِهِ ۖ فَبَاءُوا بِغَضَبٍ عَلَىٰ غَضَبٍ ۚ وَلِلْكَافِرِينَ عَذَابٌ مُّهِينٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse highlights the spiritual loss of those who denied the Qur'an and reveals the underlying motive behind their rejection. They exchanged faith for disbelief at a worthless price. The main reason for their denial, according to the verse, was envy: they resented that Allah, in His grace, chose Prophet Muhammad, one of His servants, to receive the revelation. Their refusal to accept someone chosen by Allah reflects arrogance and narrow-mindedness. As a result, they incurred Allah’s wrath more than once. Their envy ultimately led them to rejection, earning them a humiliating punishment. This verse exposes the internal spiritual corruption—envy—that fueled their outward denial and emphasizes its serious consequences.

Verse 91

وَإِذَا قِيلَ لَهُمْ آمِنُوا بِمَا أَنزَلَ اللَّهُ قَالُوا نُؤْمِنُ بِمَا أُنزِلَ عَلَيْنَا وَيَكْفُرُونَ بِمَا وَرَاءَهُ وَهُوَ الْحَقُّ مُصَدِّقًا لِّمَا مَعَهُمْ ۗ قُلْ فَلِمَ تَقْتُلُونَ أَنبِيَاءَ اللَّهِ مِن قَبْلُ إِن كُنتُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse presents the argument used by some among the Children of Israel when called upon to believe in the Qur'an: "We believe in what was sent down to us," referring to their own scriptures. However, as the verse points out, they rejected everything else, including the Qur'an, even though it is the truth and confirms the revelations they already possess—highlighting the inconsistency in their stance. Prophet Muhammad is instructed to challenge their claim by asking, "Why then did you kill Allah’s prophets before, if you were true believers?" This powerful question, based on their own history, directly contradicts their claim of faith in their scriptures and prophets, preparing the way for the following verses which give specific examples.

Verse 92

وَلَقَدْ جَاءَكُم مُّوسَىٰ بِالْبَيِّنَاتِ ثُمَّ اتَّخَذْتُمُ الْعِجْلَ مِن بَعْدِهِ وَأَنتُمْ ظَالِمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Continuing the challenge posed in the previous verse ("Why then have you killed Allah's prophets... if you were true believers?"), this verse provides a specific example of their historical behavior that contradicts their claim of faith in what was sent to them. It reminds them that Prophet Moses came to them with clear signs and proofs of his prophethood and Allah's message. Despite this, after Moses left them temporarily, they worshipped the calf. This act of worshipping something other than Allah, while Prophet Moses was still alive and after receiving clear guidance, fundamentally undermines their claim of believing in the revelations given to them. This serves as strong evidence against their assertion in Verse 91.

Verse 93

وَإِذْ أَخَذْنَا مِيثَاقَكُمْ وَرَفَعْنَا فَوْقَكُمُ الطُّورَ خُذُوا مَا آتَيْنَاكُم بِقُوَّةٍ وَاسْمَعُوا ۖ قَالُوا سَمِعْنَا وَعَصَيْنَا وَأُشْرِبُوا فِي قُلُوبِهِمُ الْعِجْلَ بِكُفْرِهِمْ ۚ قُلْ بِئْسَمَا يَأْمُرُكُم بِهِ إِيمَانُكُمْ إِن كُنتُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse presents another historical example involving Prophet Moses that challenges the claim of faith made in Verse 91. It recalls the solemn covenant Allah took with them at Mount Sinai, where the mountain was raised above them as a powerful reminder to accept the divine law with seriousness and obedience. Despite their outward commitment, their true attitude was revealed in the statement: "We hear, and we disobey." The verse further explains that their earlier worship of the calf left a deep mark on their hearts, reflecting how their lack of faith allowed love for the idol to become deeply rooted. Prophet Muhammad is then instructed to declare how terrible the consequences of such "faith" must be if it leads to disobedience and contradiction. This verse further exposes the shallowness of their claimed faith by referencing their historical actions and mindset, setting the stage for the next verse’s challenge about the hereafter.

Verse 94

قُلْ إِن كَانَتْ لَكُمُ الدَّارُ الْآخِرَةُ عِندَ اللَّهِ خَالِصَةً مِّن دُونِ النَّاسِ فَتَمَنَّوُا الْمَوْتَ إِن كُنتُمْ صَادِقِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses a claim made by some among the Children of Israel—that Paradise, the final abode with Allah, is reserved exclusively for them. This assertion aimed to elevate their status and possibly weaken the faith of Muslims. In response, Prophet Muhammad is instructed to challenge their claim: “If the Last Home with Allah is truly for you alone, and not for others, then wish for death—if you are truthful." The logic is clear: if they genuinely believed they were guaranteed salvation, they would not fear death, which would bring them to their promised reward. Their reluctance to do so reveals the insincerity of their claim. The verse effectively exposes the contradiction between their words and actions—particularly in light of their history of covenant-breaking and rejection of prophets—suggesting that such behavior is inconsistent with those who are truly favored by Allah.

Verse 95

وَلَن يَتَمَنَّوْهُ أَبَدًا بِمَا قَدَّمَتْ أَيْدِيهِمْ ۗ وَاللَّهُ عَلِيمٌ بِالظَّالِمِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following a challenge in the previous verse for those who claim paradise is exclusively theirs to wish for death if their claim is true, this verse explains their unwillingness to do so. It tells us that they will never wish for death because of the deeds they have committed in this life. Their actions have led them to a state where they fear what awaits them after death. This indicates that they are aware of their wrongdoing. The verse concludes by stating that Allah has full knowledge of those who commit wrong.

Verse 96

وَلَتَجِدَنَّهُمْ أَحْرَصَ النَّاسِ عَلَىٰ حَيَاةٍ وَمِنَ الَّذِينَ أَشْرَكُوا ۚ يَوَدُّ أَحَدُهُمْ لَوْ يُعَمَّرُ أَلْفَ سَنَةٍ وَمَا هُوَ بِمُزَحْزِحِهِ مِنَ الْعَذَابِ أَن يُعَمَّرَ ۗ وَاللَّهُ بَصِيرٌ بِمَا يَعْمَلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Continuing from the previous verse about their fear of death, this verse highlights their strong desire for worldly life. It points out that they are among the most attached to life, even more so than people who do not believe in Allah. It mentions that some might wish to live for a thousand years, but emphasizes that a long life will not protect them from punishment. The verse concludes by affirming Allah's complete awareness of everything they do, reinforcing that their actions are known and accounted for.

Verse 97

قُلْ مَن كَانَ عَدُوًّا لِّجِبْرِيلَ فَإِنَّهُ نَزَّلَهُ عَلَىٰ قَلْبِكَ بِإِذْنِ اللَّهِ مُصَدِّقًا لِّمَا بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ وَهُدًى وَبُشْرَىٰ لِلْمُؤْمِنِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses a specific claim made by some that the Angel Gabriel was their enemy. It instructs Prophet Muhammad to state clearly that Gabriel delivers the revelation (the Qur'an) to his heart by the permission of Allah. It clarifies that the Qur'an confirms the scriptures revealed before it and serves as a source of guidance and good news for those who believe. This challenges the idea of enmity towards an angel who acts purely by divine command.

Verse 98

مَن كَانَ عَدُوًّا لِّلَّهِ وَمَلَائِكَتِهِ وَرُسُلِهِ وَجِبْرِيلَ وَمِيكَالَ فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ عَدُوٌّ لِّلْكَافِرِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Building on the previous verse which addressed the claim about enmity towards Gabriel, this verse broadens the principle. It states that anyone who shows enmity towards Allah, His angels, His messengers, and specifically mentions Gabriel and Michael, will find that Allah is an enemy to those who reject faith. It underscores the unity of belief in Allah, His messengers, and His angels, and warns that opposing any of them is a sign of rejecting faith itself, which incurs Allah's displeasure.

Verse 99

وَلَقَدْ أَنزَلْنَا إِلَيْكَ آيَاتٍ بَيِّنَاتٍ ۖ وَمَا يَكْفُرُ بِهَا إِلَّا الْفَاسِقُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse reassures Prophet Muhammad about the truth of the revelations he has received, describing them as clear signs. It then characterizes those who reject these clear signs as "perverse" or evildoers. This implies that rejecting such clear truth is not due to lack of clarity in the message, but rather stems from a negative disposition or resistance to accepting it. It sets the stage for the following verses which detail some of the actions stemming from this rejection.

Verse 100

أَوَكُلَّمَا عَاهَدُوا عَهْدًا نَّبَذَهُ فَرِيقٌ مِّنْهُم ۚ بَلْ أَكْثَرُهُمْ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the description of those who reject clear signs as perverse, this verse highlights a specific behavior attributed to some among the people of the Book (referring to the Israelites in this context): breaking covenants. It asks rhetorically if it is not true that whenever they make a solemn pledge, some of them break it. The verse then asserts that the reality is that most of them lack faith, suggesting that this lack of faith is the underlying reason for their inconsistency and tendency to break promises. This point leads into the next verse which gives a clear example of this behavior.

Verse 101

وَلَمَّا جَاءَهُمْ رَسُولٌ مِّنْ عِندِ اللَّهِ مُصَدِّقٌ لِّمَا مَعَهُمْ نَبَذَ فَرِيقٌ مِّنَ الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا الْكِتَابَ كِتَابَ اللَّهِ وَرَاءَ ظُهُورِهِمْ كَأَنَّهُمْ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse provides a specific example of the behavior mentioned in the previous verse – the breaking of covenants and rejection of truth. It describes what happened when a messenger from Allah (Prophet Muhammad), confirming the scriptures they already possessed, came to them. Some of the people of the Book rejected this new scripture (the Qur'an), metaphorically "throwing the Book of Allah behind their backs." This is depicted as a careless and dismissive act, as if they were entirely unfamiliar with such divine guidance, despite their history of receiving revelation. This shows a rejection not just of the new message, but implicitly, of the principles within their own scriptures that mandated believing in later messengers.

Verse 102

وَاتَّبَعُوا مَا تَتْلُو الشَّيَاطِينُ عَلَىٰ مُلْكِ سُلَيْمَانَ ۖ وَمَا كَفَرَ سُلَيْمَانُ وَلَـٰكِنَّ الشَّيَاطِينَ كَفَرُوا يُعَلِّمُونَ النَّاسَ السِّحْرَ وَمَا أُنزِلَ عَلَى الْمَلَكَيْنِ بِبَابِلَ هَارُوتَ وَمَارُوتَ ۚ وَمَا يُعَلِّمَانِ مِنْ أَحَدٍ حَتَّىٰ يَقُولَا إِنَّمَا نَحْنُ فِتْنَةٌ فَلَا تَكْفُرْ ۖ فَيَتَعَلَّمُونَ مِنْهُمَا مَا يُفَرِّقُونَ بِهِ بَيْنَ الْمَرْءِ وَزَوْجِهِ ۚ وَمَا هُم بِضَارِّينَ بِهِ مِنْ أَحَدٍ إِلَّا بِإِذْنِ اللَّهِ ۚ وَيَتَعَلَّمُونَ مَا يَضُرُّهُمْ وَلَا يَنفَعُهُمْ ۚ وَلَقَدْ عَلِمُوا لَمَنِ اشْتَرَاهُ مَا لَهُ فِي الْآخِرَةِ مِنْ خَلَاقٍ ۚ وَلَبِئْسَ مَا شَرَوْا بِهِ أَنفُسَهُمْ ۚ لَوْ كَانُوا يَعْلَمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Instead of accepting the divine revelation of the Qur’an, which confirmed their own scriptures, this verse explains that some among them chose to follow myths and falsehoods associated with Prophet Solomon—specifically tales spread by devils who falsely claimed he practiced magic. The verse makes clear that Prophet Solomon did not engage in disbelief or magic; rather, it was the devils who committed blasphemy by teaching magic to people. It refers to a historical episode involving two angels, Harut and Marut, in Babylon. These angels were sent as a test and clearly warned people not to learn magic or fall into disbelief. Despite the warning, some learned harmful practices, such as creating conflict between spouses. However, the verse emphasizes a key principle: nothing can cause harm except by Allah’s permission. Those who pursued such knowledge only harmed themselves, gaining no real benefit—especially not in the Hereafter. They were fully aware that those who engage in such dealings would have no share in the reward of the afterlife. In doing so, they effectively sold their souls for a miserable price.

Verse 103

وَلَوْ أَنَّهُمْ آمَنُوا وَاتَّقَوْا لَمَثُوبَةٌ مِّنْ عِندِ اللَّهِ خَيْرٌ ۖ لَّوْ كَانُوا يَعْلَمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the description of the harmful path taken by some, involving magic and disbelief, this verse presents the alternative. It states that if they had maintained their faith in Allah and His messengers, and guarded themselves against evil (specifically the evil described in the previous verse, like magic and following falsehoods), the reward they would have received from Allah would have been far better than anything they gained or sought through their actions. The phrase "if they but knew" implies that they did not fully grasp the immense value of faith and righteousness compared to the worthless price for which they sold their souls, as mentioned in the previous verse.

Verse 104

يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لَا تَقُولُوا رَاعِنَا وَقُولُوا انظُرْنَا وَاسْمَعُوا ۗ وَلِلْكَافِرِينَ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse guides believers on how to address Prophet Muhammad (peace be upon him). It instructs them to use clear and respectful language, specifically advising against using words that could be misinterpreted or twisted to convey disrespect. This advice was given in the context of some individuals from other communities using ambiguous words with potentially offensive double meanings when speaking to the Prophet. This verse highlights Allah's care and protection for the Prophet and the believers against such subtle hostility. It also includes a warning of severe consequences for those who deny the truth.

Verse 105

مَّا يَوَدُّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا مِنْ أَهْلِ الْكِتَابِ وَلَا الْمُشْرِكِينَ أَن يُنَزَّلَ عَلَيْكُم مِّنْ خَيْرٍ مِّن رَّبِّكُمْ ۗ وَاللَّهُ يَخْتَصُّ بِرَحْمَتِهِ مَن يَشَاءُ ۚ وَاللَّهُ ذُو الْفَضْلِ الْعَظِيمِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the instruction on respectful communication in the previous verse, this verse addresses the attitude of some individuals from other religious groups (referred to as People of the Book) and idolaters towards the Muslims. It states that, from the perspective presented in the Qur'an, some among these groups did not wish for Muslims to receive blessings or revelations from Allah. This is stemming from a sense of resentment or envy that Allah had chosen Prophet Muhammad and his followers for His final message. The verse affirms that Allah bestows His mercy and grace upon whom He chooses, emphasizing His boundless generosity, which is seen by Muslims as the greatest blessing.

Verse 106

مَا نَنسَخْ مِنْ آيَةٍ أَوْ نُنسِهَا نَأْتِ بِخَيْرٍ مِّنْهَا أَوْ مِثْلِهَا ۗ أَلَمْ تَعْلَمْ أَنَّ اللَّهَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَيْءٍ قَدِيرٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses concerns raised about changes to certain religious rulings or revelations during the time of Prophet Muhammad. Critics questioned the divine origin of Islam, especially in light of changes such as the shift in the direction of prayer (qiblah). The verse responds by affirming that Allah has the authority to replace or modify His revelations with something better or similar, emphasizing His absolute power and wisdom in guiding humanity. This principle is presented as a definitive answer to dispel such doubts.

Verse 107

أَلَمْ تَعْلَمْ أَنَّ اللَّهَ لَهُ مُلْكُ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالْأَرْضِ ۗ وَمَا لَكُم مِّن دُونِ اللَّهِ مِن وَلِيٍّ وَلَا نَصِيرٍ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Building on the concept of Allah's absolute authority mentioned in the previous verse regarding the replacement of revelations, this verse further emphasizes Allah's complete control and ownership over all creation – the heavens and the earth. It reminds believers that Allah alone is their protector and helper. This serves as a reminder and a potential caution to Muslims, perhaps indicating that some were being affected by the criticisms from other groups and might be looking elsewhere for support or reassurance.

Verse 108

أَمْ تُرِيدُونَ أَن تَسْأَلُوا رَسُولَكُمْ كَمَا سُئِلَ مُوسَىٰ مِن قَبْلُ ۗ وَمَن يَتَبَدَّلِ الْكُفْرَ بِالْإِيمَانِ فَقَدْ ضَلَّ سَوَاءَ السَّبِيلِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse cautions Muslims not to question Prophet Muhammad in the same manner the people of Prophet Moses questioned him in the past. Earlier parts of the surah describe the persistent questioning and disobedience of the Israelites toward Moses, serving as a warning against repeating such behavior. The verse ends by stating clearly that abandoning faith after having believed is a serious deviation from the right path.

Verse 109

وَدَّ كَثِيرٌ مِّنْ أَهْلِ الْكِتَابِ لَوْ يَرُدُّونَكُم مِّن بَعْدِ إِيمَانِكُمْ كُفَّارًا حَسَدًا مِّنْ عِندِ أَنفُسِهِم مِّن بَعْدِ مَا تَبَيَّنَ لَهُمُ الْحَقُّ ۖ فَاعْفُوا وَاصْفَحُوا حَتَّىٰ يَأْتِيَ اللَّهُ بِأَمْرِهِ ۗ إِنَّ اللَّهَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَيْءٍ قَدِيرٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Returning to the theme of the attitudes of other religious groups, this verse highlights that, from the Qur'anic perspective, many among the People of the Book wished for Muslims to abandon their faith and return to disbelief. This desire is portrayed as stemming from deep-rooted resentment and envy, even after the truth of Islam had become clear to them. In response to such hostility and scheming, the verse instructs Muslims to show forgiveness and patience, trusting that Allah will ultimately bring about His will, reaffirming His absolute power.

Verse 110

وَأَقِيمُوا الصَّلَاةَ وَآتُوا الزَّكَاةَ ۚ وَمَا تُقَدِّمُوا لِأَنفُسِكُم مِّنْ خَيْرٍ تَجِدُوهُ عِندَ اللَّهِ ۗ إِنَّ اللَّهَ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ بَصِيرٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the instructions to deal with external challenges through patience and forgiveness, this verse directs believers towards strengthening their internal faith and community by emphasizing core Islamic practices. It commands steadfastness in prayer and giving regular charity (zakat). These practices are ways for Muslims to connect with Allah and invest their efforts in actions that will benefit them in the afterlife. The verse provides reassurance that all good deeds are known to and recorded by Allah, encouraging Muslims to focus on their relationship with Him and their righteous conduct as a means of resilience and spiritual growth.

Verse 111

وَقَالُوا لَن يَدْخُلَ الْجَنَّةَ إِلَّا مَن كَانَ هُودًا أَوْ نَصَارَىٰ ۗ تِلْكَ أَمَانِيُّهُمْ ۗ قُلْ هَاتُوا بُرْهَانَكُمْ إِن كُنتُمْ صَادِقِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Moving from instructions for believers, the Sura now addresses claims made by some individuals from Jewish and Christian communities regarding salvation. This verse states that these individuals claimed that only those who identified as Jews or Christians would enter Paradise. The verse dismisses these claims as mere assertions and challenges them to produce proof or evidence to support their statement, highlighting the principle that such significant claims require substantiation.

Verse 112

بَلَىٰ مَنْ أَسْلَمَ وَجْهَهُ لِلَّهِ وَهُوَ مُحْسِنٌ فَلَهُ أَجْرُهُ عِندَ رَبِّهِ وَلَا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse directly refutes the exclusive claims made in the previous verse (111) and establishes a fundamental principle in Islam regarding salvation and reward. It states that anyone who completely submits themselves to Allah and performs good deeds will receive their reward from their Lord. Salvation is based on sincere submission to Allah and righteous actions, not on affiliation with a particular group or lineage. It emphasizes that true faith is expressed through both internal conviction and outward good deeds, leading to a state where believers will have no fear or grief in the Hereafter.

Verse 113

وَقَالَتِ الْيَهُودُ لَيْسَتِ النَّصَارَىٰ عَلَىٰ شَيْءٍ وَقَالَتِ النَّصَارَىٰ لَيْسَتِ الْيَهُودُ عَلَىٰ شَيْءٍ وَهُمْ يَتْلُونَ الْكِتَابَ ۗ كَذَٰلِكَ قَالَ الَّذِينَ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ مِثْلَ قَوْلِهِمْ ۚ فَاللَّهُ يَحْكُمُ بَيْنَهُمْ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ فِيمَا كَانُوا فِيهِ يَخْتَلِفُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse highlights the disagreements and mutual criticisms between some members of the Jewish and Christian communities, where each accused the other of lacking a true foundation for their beliefs. Yet both groups claimed to follow scriptures revealed by Allah. The verse also draws a parallel between these disputes and the remarks of those "without knowledge" (understood as the Arab idolaters), who, observing these conflicts, dismissed both groups altogether. It concludes by affirming that Allah will be the ultimate judge of these disputes on the Day of Judgment, underscoring that the final authority in such matters belongs to Allah alone.

Verse 114

وَمَنْ أَظْلَمُ مِمَّن مَّنَعَ مَسَاجِدَ اللَّهِ أَن يُذْكَرَ فِيهَا اسْمُهُ وَسَعَىٰ فِي خَرَابِهَا ۚ أُولَـٰئِكَ مَا كَانَ لَهُمْ أَن يَدْخُلُوهَا إِلَّا خَائِفِينَ ۚ لَهُمْ فِي الدُّنْيَا خِزْيٌ وَلَهُمْ فِي الْآخِرَةِ عَذَابٌ عَظِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse highlights the great injustice of those who prevent others from remembering Allah's name in places of worship or who actively seek to damage or destroy these places. It explains that such actions are deeply wrong and those who commit them deserve consequences both in this life and the next.

Verse 115

وَلِلَّهِ الْمَشْرِقُ وَالْمَغْرِبُ ۚ فَأَيْنَمَا تُوَلُّوا فَثَمَّ وَجْهُ اللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّ اللَّهَ وَاسِعٌ عَلِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse continues the discussion of places and directions of worship, emphasizing Allah's boundless nature: east, west, north, south—all directions and places belong to Him. It offers reassurance regarding the legitimacy of the prayer direction, especially in light of the change Muslims experienced in this regard. The verse clarifies that such changes are not grounds for dispute; wherever one turns in sincere devotion, they are turning toward Allah. It concludes by affirming Allah's limitless bounty and His complete knowledge of all things, assuring that sincere worship, regardless of direction, is known and accepted by Him. In the Qur’an, expressions such as "there is the presence of Allah" or "the face of Allah" appear in reference to Allah, in ways that are unique to Him. Muslims accept these terms as they are revealed, without likening Allah to His creation or attempting to define their exact nature. His attributes are understood in a way that is appropriate to His majesty and are beyond human comprehension.

Verse 116

وَقَالُوا اتَّخَذَ اللَّهُ وَلَدًا ۗ سُبْحَانَهُ ۖ بَل لَّهُ مَا فِي السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالْأَرْضِ ۖ كُلٌّ لَّهُ قَانِتُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses a misconception held by some groups who claimed that Allah has a son. This idea was present among Christians, some Jews who held a similar belief about Prophet Ezra, and some Arab idolaters who considered angels to be daughters of Allah. The verse strongly refutes this idea, declaring "Glory be to Him!" to emphasize Allah's absolute transcendence and freedom from any human-like relationships or limitations. It asserts that everything in the heavens and on earth belongs to Allah and is subservient to Him, highlighting His unique sovereignty and the fact that He does not need offspring or partners.

Verse 117

بَدِيعُ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالْأَرْضِ ۖ وَإِذَا قَضَىٰ أَمْرًا فَإِنَّمَا يَقُولُ لَهُ كُن فَيَكُونُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Continuing the theme of Allah's unique nature and power, this verse further explains that Allah is the sole Originator of everything in the universe. It describes His absolute power of creation: when He decides something is to exist, He simply says "Be," and it comes into being instantly. This reflects Allah's direct, absolute, and omnipotent will in creation, without needing intermediaries. This concept reinforces the idea of Allah's uniqueness and refutes any notion of Him having a son or partners, as His creative power is absolute and immediate.

Verse 118

وَقَالَ الَّذِينَ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ لَوْلَا يُكَلِّمُنَا اللَّهُ أَوْ تَأْتِينَا آيَةٌ ۗ كَذَٰلِكَ قَالَ الَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِم مِّثْلَ قَوْلِهِمْ ۘ تَشَابَهَتْ قُلُوبُهُمْ ۗ قَدْ بَيَّنَّا الْآيَاتِ لِقَوْمٍ يُوقِنُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse speaks about people who lack true understanding and challenge why Allah does not speak directly to them or show them specific miraculous signs. This attitude is not new and that previous nations made similar demands of their prophets, such as when the Israelites asked Prophet Moses to see Allah directly. The verse points out that the hearts and attitudes of those who make such demands are similar across different times. It then clarifies that Allah has indeed provided clear signs, but these signs are most apparent and beneficial to those who already possess sincere faith and conviction in their hearts. Faith enables one to recognize and appreciate the meaning and purpose of these signs.

Verse 119

إِنَّا أَرْسَلْنَاكَ بِالْحَقِّ بَشِيرًا وَنَذِيرًا ۖ وَلَا تُسْأَلُ عَنْ أَصْحَابِ الْجَحِيمِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Addressing Prophet Muhammad, this verse defines his role and responsibilities. It states that he was sent with the truth as a bringer of good news to those who believe and obey Allah, and as a warner to those who reject His message. The Prophet’s mission is to convey this message, but he is not accountable for the ultimate fate of those who disbelieve and face punishment.

Verse 120

وَلَن تَرْضَىٰ عَنكَ الْيَهُودُ وَلَا النَّصَارَىٰ حَتَّىٰ تَتَّبِعَ مِلَّتَهُمْ ۗ قُلْ إِنَّ هُدَى اللَّهِ هُوَ الْهُدَىٰ ۗ وَلَئِنِ اتَّبَعْتَ أَهْوَاءَهُم بَعْدَ الَّذِي جَاءَكَ مِنَ الْعِلْمِ ۙ مَا لَكَ مِنَ اللَّهِ مِن وَلِيٍّ وَلَا نَصِيرٍ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse reveals the deep-seated desire of some among the Jews and Christians during that time for Prophet Muhammad to abandon his faith and adopt theirs. It states that they would not be satisfied with him unless he followed their religious path. The verse instructs the Prophet to state clearly that "The Guidance of Allah—that is the (only) Guidance," emphasizing that the true and complete guidance comes solely from Allah. It sternly warns the Prophet (and by extension, Muslims) against compromising their faith or following the desires of others after having received divine knowledge, stating that doing so would leave them without protection or help from Allah.

Verse 121

الَّذِينَ آتَيْنَاهُمُ الْكِتَابَ يَتْلُونَهُ حَقَّ تِلَاوَتِهِ أُولَـٰئِكَ يُؤْمِنُونَ بِهِ ۗ وَمَن يَكْفُرْ بِهِ فَأُولَـٰئِكَ هُمُ الْخَاسِرُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the warning in the previous verse, this verse highlights the sincere and pious individuals among the People of the Book. These are those who recite the Scripture with honesty and genuine intent, and are therefore more inclined to accept the truth when they recognize it. In contrast, those who persist in rejecting faith are described as the true losers.

Verse 122

يَا بَنِي إِسْرَائِيلَ اذْكُرُوا نِعْمَتِيَ الَّتِي أَنْعَمْتُ عَلَيْكُمْ وَأَنِّي فَضَّلْتُكُمْ عَلَى الْعَالَمِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse is a direct address to the Children of Israel (descendants of Prophet Jacob, also known as Israel). It reminds them of the many favors and blessings Allah has bestowed upon them throughout their history. This appeal urges them to remember their special status as recipients and carriers of Allah’s message. This reminder sets the stage for the warning that follows.

Verse 123

وَاتَّقُوا يَوْمًا لَّا تَجْزِي نَفْسٌ عَن نَّفْسٍ شَيْئًا وَلَا يُقْبَلُ مِنْهَا عَدْلٌ وَلَا تَنفَعُهَا شَفَاعَةٌ وَلَا هُمْ يُنصَرُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Concluding the appeal to the Children of Israel and the broader discussion, this verse delivers a solemn warning about the Day of Judgment. It emphasizes that on that Day, every soul will be accountable for its own deeds. No soul can help another, no ransom or compensation will be accepted, intercession will only benefit with Allah’s permission, and no external aid will be available. This serves as a reminder against the belief held by some among the Children of Israel that their descent from Abraham would "ransom" them on the Day of Judgment—a belief refuted in the following verse.

Verse 124

وَإِذِ ابْتَلَىٰ إِبْرَاهِيمَ رَبُّهُ بِكَلِمَاتٍ فَأَتَمَّهُنَّ ۖ قَالَ إِنِّي جَاعِلُكَ لِلنَّاسِ إِمَامًا ۖ قَالَ وَمِن ذُرِّيَّتِي ۖ قَالَ لَا يَنَالُ عَهْدِي الظَّالِمِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse reminds us of Prophet Abraham, a highly respected figure in Islam, who was tested by Allah with specific commands and successfully fulfilled them. The Qur’an details the severe trials Abraham endured. After these tests, Allah promised to make him a leader for all humanity, a model of righteousness to be followed. Abraham, being human, asked whether this leadership would extend to his descendants. Allah replied that the promise applies only to those among his offspring who do good. This emphasizes a core principle: closeness to Allah and leadership in faith depend on sincere belief and righteous deeds, not merely on family lineage. Those who turn away from righteousness, associate partners with Allah, or cause harm are excluded from this promise. Thus, the divine promise of leadership applies only to Abraham’s righteous descendants, excluding wrongdoers—including the misguided Israelites and the polytheistic Ishmaelites.

Verse 125

وَإِذْ جَعَلْنَا الْبَيْتَ مَثَابَةً لِّلنَّاسِ وَأَمْنًا وَاتَّخِذُوا مِن مَّقَامِ إِبْرَاهِيمَ مُصَلًّى ۖ وَعَهِدْنَا إِلَىٰ إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَإِسْمَاعِيلَ أَن طَهِّرَا بَيْتِيَ لِلطَّائِفِينَ وَالْعَاكِفِينَ وَالرُّكَّعِ السُّجُودِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the mention of Prophet Abraham's status, this verse speaks about the Ka'bah in Makkah, a central and sacred building for Muslims. It tells us that Allah designated this House as a gathering place and a sanctuary for people, where they should find peace and security. Muslims are instructed to take the place where Prophet Abraham stood as a place for prayer. The verse also mentions a covenant made with Prophet Abraham and his son, Prophet Ishmael. They were tasked with purifying this House, making it ready and sacred for those who would come to perform various acts of worship there, such as circling it, staying for spiritual devotion, or praying by bowing and prostrating. This shows the historical significance of the Ka'bah as a place dedicated to the worship of Allah from the time of Prophet Abraham and Prophet Ishmael.

Verse 126

وَإِذْ قَالَ إِبْرَاهِيمُ رَبِّ اجْعَلْ هَـٰذَا بَلَدًا آمِنًا وَارْزُقْ أَهْلَهُ مِنَ الثَّمَرَاتِ مَنْ آمَنَ مِنْهُم بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ ۖ قَالَ وَمَن كَفَرَ فَأُمَتِّعُهُ قَلِيلًا ثُمَّ أَضْطَرُّهُ إِلَىٰ عَذَابِ النَّارِ ۖ وَبِئْسَ الْمَصِيرُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse continues the account related to the Ka'bah and the city of Makkah. Prophet Abraham prays for the city to become a place of peace and security, asking Allah to provide for "those among them who believe in Allah and the Last Day." Allah states that He will grant enjoyment in this life even to those who reject faith, but their final destination will be the Fire. When Abraham inquired about who deserved world leadership, it was made clear that only his truly believing and righteous children—excluding the wrongdoers—were worthy. Allah clarified the difference between religious leadership and worldly provision. Only the faithful are entitled to religious leadership, while both believers and unbelievers may receive provision in this life. Therefore, abundant material wealth should not be mistaken as a sign of Allah’s pleasure or divine appointment for religious guidance. This verse links to the previous ones by showing Abraham’s concern for the future of Makkah and its people after he and Prophet Ishmael built the Ka'bah.

Verse 127

وَإِذْ يَرْفَعُ إِبْرَاهِيمُ الْقَوَاعِدَ مِنَ الْبَيْتِ وَإِسْمَاعِيلُ رَبَّنَا تَقَبَّلْ مِنَّا ۖ إِنَّكَ أَنتَ السَّمِيعُ الْعَلِيمُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse brings us directly into the scene of Prophet Abraham and Prophet Ishmael working together to lay the foundations of the Ka'bah. While engaged in this momentous task, they offered a heartfelt prayer. Their first request was that Allah accept this service from them. They acknowledged Allah as the All-Hearing and All-Knowing, recognizing that He hears their prayer and is fully aware of their intentions and efforts. This reflects their humility and dedication to performing the work solely for Allah’s sake, hoping for His acceptance. This prayer begins a series of supplications recorded in the following verses, all made during the building of the sacred House.

Verse 128

رَبَّنَا وَاجْعَلْنَا مُسْلِمَيْنِ لَكَ وَمِن ذُرِّيَّتِنَا أُمَّةً مُّسْلِمَةً لَّكَ وَأَرِنَا مَنَاسِكَنَا وَتُبْ عَلَيْنَا ۖ إِنَّكَ أَنتَ التَّوَّابُ الرَّحِيمُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Continuing their prayer while building the Ka'bah, Prophet Abraham and Prophet Ishmael asked Allah to make them people who submit fully to His will – this is the meaning of "Muslim" in Arabic. They also prayed for their descendants to be a community that submits to Allah's will. This shows their deep concern that the faith would continue through their lineage. They further asked Allah to guide them and show them the correct ways to perform the religious rites. Finally, they prayed for Allah's mercy and forgiveness, acknowledging that Allah is the Oft-Returning (accepting repentance) and Most Merciful. This part of the prayer emphasizes the importance of submission to Allah and the desire for future generations to uphold this faith.

Verse 129

رَبَّنَا وَابْعَثْ فِيهِمْ رَسُولًا مِّنْهُمْ يَتْلُو عَلَيْهِمْ آيَاتِكَ وَيُعَلِّمُهُمُ الْكِتَابَ وَالْحِكْمَةَ وَيُزَكِّيهِمْ ۚ إِنَّكَ أَنتَ الْعَزِيزُ الْحَكِيمُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Concluding their prayer while building the Ka'bah, Prophet Abraham and Prophet Ishmael made a specific request for their future descendants. They asked Allah to send a Messenger from among their own people who would recite Allah's revelations to them, teach them the scripture and wisdom, and purify them. They acknowledged Allah's power and wisdom in this request, stating He is the Exalted in Might and the Wise. Muslims understand this prayer as a prophecy and a request for the coming of Prophet Muhammad, who was a direct descendant of Prophet Ishmael and was sent to teach and purify humanity. This prayer, made during the building of the Ka'bah, highlights the continuity of the message of submission to Allah through the line of Abraham and Ishmael.

Verse 130

وَمَن يَرْغَبُ عَن مِّلَّةِ إِبْرَاهِيمَ إِلَّا مَن سَفِهَ نَفْسَهُ ۚ وَلَقَدِ اصْطَفَيْنَاهُ فِي الدُّنْيَا ۖ وَإِنَّهُ فِي الْآخِرَةِ لَمِنَ الصَّالِحِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse shifts focus slightly to emphasize the importance and clarity of Prophet Abraham's faith. It asks rhetorically who would turn away from his religion except someone who has foolishly degraded themselves. Prophet Abraham's religion was fundamentally about pure submission to Allah, which is what the word "Islam" means. The verse reaffirms Prophet Abraham's high status, stating that Allah chose him, purified him in this life, and that he will be among the righteous in the Hereafter. This serves as a strong statement about the true nature of Prophet Abraham's faith, contrasting it with any later deviations or claims.

Verse 131

إِذْ قَالَ لَهُ رَبُّهُ أَسْلِمْ ۖ قَالَ أَسْلَمْتُ لِرَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the statement about Prophet Abraham's faith, this verse gives a direct example of his pure submission. It recounts a moment when Allah commanded him to "Submit" or "Bow your will to Me." Prophet Abraham's immediate and complete response was, "I submit my will to the Lord of the Universe." This simple yet profound exchange perfectly illustrates the essence of his faith – total and willing surrender to the Creator and Sustainer of everything that exists.

Verse 132

وَوَصَّىٰ بِهَا إِبْرَاهِيمُ بَنِيهِ وَيَعْقُوبُ يَا بَنِيَّ إِنَّ اللَّهَ اصْطَفَىٰ لَكُمُ الدِّينَ فَلَا تَمُوتُنَّ إِلَّا وَأَنتُم مُّسْلِمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse shows how the faith of pure submission (Islam) was passed down through generations. It states that Prophet Abraham left this faith as a legacy to his sons. Similarly, his grandson, Prophet Jacob (also known as Israel), left the same counsel to his children. Their message was a strong urging: "Allah has chosen this Faith for you; therefore, make sure you do not die except in a state of submission to Allah." This emphasizes that the religion of submission was not just for them, but was intended to be a continuous inheritance for their descendants, stressing its divine origin and importance until the very end of life. This connects directly to Prophet Abraham's own example of submission shown in the previous verse.

Verse 133

أَمْ كُنتُمْ شُهَدَاءَ إِذْ حَضَرَ يَعْقُوبَ الْمَوْتُ إِذْ قَالَ لِبَنِيهِ مَا تَعْبُدُونَ مِن بَعْدِي قَالُوا نَعْبُدُ إِلَـٰهَكَ وَإِلَـٰهَ آبَائِكَ إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَإِسْمَاعِيلَ وَإِسْحَاقَ إِلَـٰهًا وَاحِدًا وَنَحْنُ لَهُ مُسْلِمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse paints a powerful and moving scene of Prophet Jacob (also known as Israel) on his deathbed. Addressing people who lived much later, the verse uses a rhetorical question, "Were you witnesses...?", to emphasize the truth of the event it describes. Prophet Jacob's greatest concern as his life was ending was the faith his children would follow. He asked them directly, "Whom will you worship after me?" Their reply was a source of great reassurance: they declared they would worship the same God he worshipped, the God of his fathers, Prophet Abraham, Prophet Ishmael, and Prophet Isaac – the one true Allah. They affirmed their commitment, saying, "To Him we bow (in Islam)," meaning they submit to Allah. This verse shows the strong chain of faith passed down from Prophet Abraham through Prophet Isaac and Prophet Jacob, with the children affirming their commitment to the pure submission to Allah, linking them to the legacy mentioned in the previous verse.

Verse 134

تِلْكَ أُمَّةٌ قَدْ خَلَتْ ۖ لَهَا مَا كَسَبَتْ وَلَكُم مَّا كَسَبْتُمْ ۖ وَلَا تُسْأَلُونَ عَمَّا كَانُوا يَعْمَلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the historical accounts of Prophet Jacob and his children affirming their commitment to Allah, this verse makes an important distinction. It emphasizes that past generations, like the descendants of Jacob previously mentioned, are accountable only for their own deeds. Their actions, whether good or bad, are recorded for them alone. Likewise, every generation is responsible for its own actions. You will not be questioned about what those before you did. This highlights a core principle in Islam: accountability is individual and not based on one's lineage or ancestral history. It also sets the stage for the discussion that follows about the true foundation of faith.

Verse 135

وَقَالُوا كُونُوا هُودًا أَوْ نَصَارَىٰ تَهْتَدُوا ۗ قُلْ بَلْ مِلَّةَ إِبْرَاهِيمَ حَنِيفًا ۖ وَمَا كَانَ مِنَ الْمُشْرِكِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Building on the principle of individual accountability rather than inherited merit, this verse addresses the claims made by some from earlier religious communities who asserted that others needed to follow their specific religious path in order to be rightly guided. Allah instructs Prophet Muhammad to respond by declaring that the true path is the way of Prophet Abraham, who was purely devoted to Allah alone and did not associate any partners with Him. This connects the faith of Muslims directly to Prophet Abraham, highlighting that the core message throughout history has been the pure worship of Allah alone.

Verse 136

قُولُوا آمَنَّا بِاللَّهِ وَمَا أُنزِلَ إِلَيْنَا وَمَا أُنزِلَ إِلَىٰ إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَإِسْمَاعِيلَ وَإِسْحَاقَ وَيَعْقُوبَ وَالْأَسْبَاطِ وَمَا أُوتِيَ مُوسَىٰ وَعِيسَىٰ وَمَا أُوتِيَ النَّبِيُّونَ مِن رَّبِّهِمْ لَا نُفَرِّقُ بَيْنَ أَحَدٍ مِّنْهُمْ وَنَحْنُ لَهُ مُسْلِمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse outlines the comprehensive belief held by Muslims. It declares belief in Allah, in the revelation given to Prophet Muhammad (the Qur'an), and in the revelations sent to all the prophets before him, including Prophet Abraham, Prophet Ishmael, Prophet Isaac, Prophet Jacob, the tribes who descended from Jacob, Prophet Moses, Prophet Jesus, and all other prophets sent by Allah. A key principle emphasized here is that Muslims believe in and respect all of Allah's prophets, making no distinction between them in terms of accepting their divine mission. This universal acceptance of all prophets and their revelations is central to Islam and signifies submission to Allah alone. The verse follows the call to follow the path of Abraham by clarifying that this includes belief in the continuity of divine guidance through all of Allah’s prophets.

Verse 137

فَإِنْ آمَنُوا بِمِثْلِ مَا آمَنتُم بِهِ فَقَدِ اهْتَدَوا ۖ وَّإِن تَوَلَّوْا فَإِنَّمَا هُمْ فِي شِقَاقٍ ۖ فَسَيَكْفِيكَهُمُ اللَّهُ ۚ وَهُوَ السَّمِيعُ الْعَلِيمُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the declaration of belief in all prophets and revelations, this verse presents the outcome of accepting or rejecting this unified understanding of faith. It states that if others accept this belief in Allah and all His messengers, as Muslims do, then they are rightly guided. However, if they turn away from this comprehensive belief, they are creating division and distancing themselves from the core truth. The verse offers reassurance by affirming that Allah is sufficient to protect the Prophet and the believers against such opposition. He is All-Hearing and All-Knowing, fully aware of their disputes.

Verse 138

صِبْغَةَ اللَّهِ ۖ وَمَنْ أَحْسَنُ مِنَ اللَّهِ صِبْغَةً ۖ وَنَحْنُ لَهُ عَابِدُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse describes faith as having a distinct "color" or "hue." It signifies that the religion originates from Allah, giving it a unique and pure quality that surpasses anything else. It is like a spiritual dye or purification that comes directly from the Divine Source. The verse poses a rhetorical question: who can provide a better 'color' or purification than Allah? This highlights that true worship and submission belong to Allah alone, the One from whom this unique spiritual identity is derived. Notably, the Qur'anic style here blends Allah's declaration of the faith’s divine origin with the believers’ affirmation of worshiping Him alone, reflecting the close and direct connection between them.

Verse 139

قُلْ أَتُحَاجُّونَنَا فِي اللَّهِ وَهُوَ رَبُّنَا وَرَبُّكُمْ وَلَنَا أَعْمَالُنَا وَلَكُمْ أَعْمَالُكُمْ وَنَحْنُ لَهُ مُخْلِصُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse continues the discussion by challenging any attempt to dispute the fundamental truth about Allah. It highlights the inconsistency of arguing over Allah, since He is universally acknowledged as the Lord of all people, whether they are Muslims or not. The verse also reinforces the principle of individual accountability, previously emphasized in Verse 134—that each person is responsible for their own actions. Additionally, it underlines the believers’ sincere devotion to Allah alone. By focusing on these shared beliefs (Allah as the universal Lord) and core Islamic principles (personal responsibility, sincere worship), the verse addresses the underlying causes of religious differences.

Verse 140

أَمْ تَقُولُونَ إِنَّ إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَإِسْمَاعِيلَ وَإِسْحَاقَ وَيَعْقُوبَ وَالْأَسْبَاطَ كَانُوا هُودًا أَوْ نَصَارَىٰ ۗ قُلْ أَأَنتُمْ أَعْلَمُ أَمِ اللَّهُ ۗ وَمَنْ أَظْلَمُ مِمَّن كَتَمَ شَهَادَةً عِندَهُ مِنَ اللَّهِ ۗ وَمَا اللَّهُ بِغَافِلٍ عَمَّا تَعْمَلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse presents a direct question to those who claim that early prophets such as Prophet Abraham, Prophet Ishmael, Prophet Isaac, Prophet Jacob, and the tribes descended from Jacob were followers of Judaism or Christianity. It points out that these prophets lived long before Judaism and Christianity emerged as distinct religions. Therefore, attributing these later religious identities to them is historically incorrect. The verse then poses a rhetorical challenge: "Do you know better than Allah?", confronting those who make such claims with the reality of divine knowledge. It further highlights the serious wrongdoing of those who knowingly conceal or distort the truth revealed by Allah, reminding them that Allah is fully aware of all that people do, including efforts to hide the truth. This verse reinforces the argument made in Verse 135, emphasizing the call to follow the pure monotheism of Abraham, distinct from later religious labels and divisions.

Verse 141

تِلْكَ أُمَّةٌ قَدْ خَلَتْ ۖ لَهَا مَا كَسَبَتْ وَلَكُم مَّا كَسَبْتُمْ ۖ وَلَا تُسْأَلُونَ عَمَّا كَانُوا يَعْمَلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse repeats the statement made in Verse 134. By repeating it after the discussion about the prophets and the claims of later religious groups, it powerfully reinforces the central theme: lineage or historical association does not guarantee salvation or special status. The deeds of those past generations are for them, and your deeds are for you. You are not responsible for their actions, nor do their merits automatically benefit you. This serves as a concluding statement to this section, bringing the argument back to the core Islamic principle of individual accountability before Allah, separate from ancestral or historical group identity.

Verse 142

سَيَقُولُ السُّفَهَاءُ مِنَ النَّاسِ مَا وَلَّاهُمْ عَن قِبْلَتِهِمُ الَّتِي كَانُوا عَلَيْهَا ۚ قُل لِّلَّهِ الْمَشْرِقُ وَالْمَغْرِبُ ۚ يَهْدِي مَن يَشَاءُ إِلَىٰ صِرَاطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse anticipates questions that people might raise about a change in the direction Muslims face during prayer. Previously, Muslims in Madinah prayed facing Jerusalem, which was also the direction for Jewish and Christian prayer. This verse refers to those who might question why Muslims would change this direction. It directs Prophet Muhammad to respond by stating that all directions, east and west, belong to Allah. This emphasizes Allah's absolute sovereignty and that He guides whomever He wills to the correct path. The significance of a direction for prayer (Qibla) comes from Allah's designation, not from any inherent quality of the direction itself. This serves to immediately address potential objections and set the stage for the subsequent verses.

Verse 143

وَكَذَٰلِكَ جَعَلْنَاكُمْ أُمَّةً وَسَطًا لِّتَكُونُوا شُهَدَاءَ عَلَى النَّاسِ وَيَكُونَ الرَّسُولُ عَلَيْكُمْ شَهِيدًا ۗ وَمَا جَعَلْنَا الْقِبْلَةَ الَّتِي كُنتَ عَلَيْهَا إِلَّا لِنَعْلَمَ مَن يَتَّبِعُ الرَّسُولَ مِمَّن يَنقَلِبُ عَلَىٰ عَقِبَيْهِ ۚ وَإِن كَانَتْ لَكَبِيرَةً إِلَّا عَلَى الَّذِينَ هَدَى اللَّهُ ۗ وَمَا كَانَ اللَّهُ لِيُضِيعَ إِيمَانَكُمْ ۚ إِنَّ اللَّهَ بِالنَّاسِ لَرَءُوفٌ رَّحِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse connects the change in the direction of prayer (Qibla) to the identity and role of the Muslim community, called an *Ummah* in the Qur'an. Allah states that He has made this community a "justly balanced Ummah." This means they are meant to be moderate, upright, and a model for humanity, positioned to bear witness to truth and justice among all people. Prophet Muhammad serves as a witness over this community, guiding and setting the standard for their actions and beliefs. The verse then clarifies that the *previous* direction of prayer (Jerusalem) was a test. It was intended to distinguish those who truly followed the Messenger from those whose faith was shallow. This change was difficult, except for those whom Allah guided. The verse reassures believers that Allah would never cause their earlier prayers or their faith to be wasted, confirming His compassion and mercy.

Verse 144

قَدْ نَرَىٰ تَقَلُّبَ وَجْهِكَ فِي السَّمَاءِ ۖ فَلَنُوَلِّيَنَّكَ قِبْلَةً تَرْضَاهَا ۚ فَوَلِّ وَجْهَكَ شَطْرَ الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ ۚ وَحَيْثُ مَا كُنتُمْ فَوَلُّوا وُجُوهَكُمْ شَطْرَهُ ۗ وَإِنَّ الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا الْكِتَابَ لَيَعْلَمُونَ أَنَّهُ الْحَقُّ مِن رَّبِّهِمْ ۗ وَمَا اللَّهُ بِغَافِلٍ عَمَّا يَعْمَلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse describes Prophet Muhammad's unspoken desire for a different direction of prayer, showing him looking towards the heavens. Allah acknowledges this desire and states that He will now turn him to a Qibla that will be pleasing to him. This new, permanent direction is specified as the Sacred Mosque (the Ka'bah) in Mecca. The instruction is universal: wherever Muslims are, they should turn their faces towards this direction in prayer. The verse adds that the people who received earlier revelations know that this designation is the truth from their Lord, implying its historical and religious significance. It also assures believers that Allah is fully aware of the actions of all. This verse marks the formal institution of the Ka'bah as the permanent Qibla for Muslims worldwide.

Verse 145

وَلَئِنْ أَتَيْتَ الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا الْكِتَابَ بِكُلِّ آيَةٍ مَّا تَبِعُوا قِبْلَتَكَ ۚ وَمَا أَنتَ بِتَابِعٍ قِبْلَتَهُمْ ۚ وَمَا بَعْضُهُم بِتَابِعٍ قِبْلَةَ بَعْضٍ ۚ وَلَئِنِ اتَّبَعْتَ أَهْوَاءَهُم مِّن بَعْدِ مَا جَاءَكَ مِنَ الْعِلْمِ ۙ إِنَّكَ إِذًا لَّمِنَ الظَّالِمِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the instruction to face the Sacred Mosque, this verse addresses the differing perspectives and challenges that arose. It states that even if Prophet Muhammad were to present every possible sign, the people of earlier revelations would not follow the Muslim Qibla. It also makes it clear that the Prophet is not to follow their Qibla. Furthermore, it highlights that even among themselves, the various groups among the people of earlier revelations do not agree on a single direction for prayer. The verse concludes with a strong warning to the Prophet (and by extension, to all Muslims) that following the desires of others after receiving clear knowledge from Allah would be a clear deviation from the right path. This emphasizes the distinct identity of the Muslim faith and the importance of adhering strictly to divine guidance.

Verse 146

الَّذِينَ آتَيْنَاهُمُ الْكِتَابَ يَعْرِفُونَهُ كَمَا يَعْرِفُونَ أَبْنَاءَهُمْ ۖ وَإِنَّ فَرِيقًا مِّنْهُمْ لَيَكْتُمُونَ الْحَقَّ وَهُمْ يَعْلَمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse asserts that those who received earlier revelations possess certain knowledge about the truth of Prophet Muhammad's message, including the significance of the Sacred Mosque—that is, the Ka'bah, which was built by Prophet Abraham. It employs a powerful comparison, stating that they recognize this truth as clearly as they recognize their own children. The phrase "to recognize something as well as one recognizes one's sons" is an Arabic idiom, used to describe knowledge that is beyond any doubt or uncertainty. Despite this inner certainty, the verse highlights that some among them deliberately conceal this truth. This explains why, as mentioned in the previous verse, they refuse to accept or follow the Muslim Qibla, despite knowing its authenticity.

Verse 147

الْحَقُّ مِن رَّبِّكَ ۖ فَلَا تَكُونَنَّ مِنَ الْمُمْتَرِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse directly addresses Prophet Muhammad, reaffirming the message from the previous verses. It confirms that what has been revealed to him is the absolute truth, originating solely from Allah. The Prophet is instructed to harbor no doubt whatsoever regarding this truth. Although the address is to the Prophet, it also serves as a reassurance to all believers and as a firm response to any doubts or challenges raised by others. The verse underscores the ultimate authority, clarity, and certainty of Allah's revelation.

Verse 148

وَلِكُلٍّ وِجْهَةٌ هُوَ مُوَلِّيهَا ۖ فَاسْتَبِقُوا الْخَيْرَاتِ ۚ أَيْنَ مَا تَكُونُوا يَأْتِ بِكُمُ اللَّهُ جَمِيعًا ۚ إِنَّ اللَّهَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَيْءٍ قَدِيرٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: After addressing the specific issue of the Qibla and the differing perspectives, this verse offers a broader perspective. It acknowledges that different communities may have different directions they turn towards. However, it then immediately shifts the focus for Muslims: instead of being distracted by these differences or engaging in futile arguments, they are urged to compete with one another in doing good deeds. This redirects their energy towards positive action and moral excellence. It reassures them that no matter where they are, Allah has the power to bring them together, emphasizing the unity of the Muslim community and Allah's complete power over all things. After addressing the specific issue of the Qibla and the differing perspectives, this verse offers a broader lesson. It acknowledges that different communities may have their own directions of prayer. However, it redirects the focus of Muslims away from becoming preoccupied with these differences or falling into unproductive arguments. Instead, they are encouraged to strive with one another in performing good deeds, channeling their energy into positive action and moral excellence. The verse further reassures them that wherever they may be, Allah has the power to gather them together, emphasizing Allah's absolute control over all things.

Verse 149

وَمِنْ حَيْثُ خَرَجْتَ فَوَلِّ وَجْهَكَ شَطْرَ الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ ۖ وَإِنَّهُ لَلْحَقُّ مِن رَّبِّكَ ۗ وَمَا اللَّهُ بِغَافِلٍ عَمَّا تَعْمَلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse reiterates the command given in Verse 144 regarding the direction of prayer. It emphasizes again, "From wherever you may come forth," meaning from any location or starting point, Prophet Muhammad and thus all Muslims are to turn their face towards the Sacred Mosque during prayer. It stresses that this is undeniably "the truth from your Lord." The verse concludes by reminding believers that Allah is fully aware of everything they do, serving as a reminder of accountability and sincerity in following His commands. This repetition underscores the importance of this instruction.

Verse 150

وَمِنْ حَيْثُ خَرَجْتَ فَوَلِّ وَجْهَكَ شَطْرَ الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ ۚ وَحَيْثُ مَا كُنتُمْ فَوَلُّوا وُجُوهَكُمْ شَطْرَهُ لِئَلَّا يَكُونَ لِلنَّاسِ عَلَيْكُمْ حُجَّةٌ إِلَّا الَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا مِنْهُمْ فَلَا تَخْشَوْهُمْ وَاخْشَوْنِي وَلِأُتِمَّ نِعْمَتِي عَلَيْكُمْ وَلَعَلَّكُمْ تَهْتَدُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This is the third time the command to face the Sacred Mosque in prayer is given, underscoring its great importance. In this repetition, the directive is extended universally to all Muslims, regardless of where they are. A key reason provided is to eliminate any argument others might have against them. By establishing a clear and divinely appointed Qibla connected to the legacy of Prophet Abraham, Muslims are given a strong foundation that leaves no room for legitimate criticism regarding their direction of worship. The verse notes that only those who persist in wrongdoing will continue to argue. It then instructs Muslims not to fear such detractors but to fear Allah alone. This guidance is tied to Allah's completion of His favor upon them and His commitment to guide them. Overall, the verse reinforces the formation of a distinct Muslim identity, rooted in obedience to Allah and supported by His protection and guidance.

Verse 151

كَمَا أَرْسَلْنَا فِيكُمْ رَسُولًا مِّنكُمْ يَتْلُو عَلَيْكُمْ آيَاتِنَا وَيُزَكِّيكُمْ وَيُعَلِّمُكُمُ الْكِتَابَ وَالْحِكْمَةَ وَيُعَلِّمُكُم مَّا لَمْ تَكُونُوا تَعْلَمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse broadens the focus while remaining connected to the theme of Allah's favor upon the Muslim community, as mentioned at the end of the previous verse. It reminds them of a great blessing already bestowed: the sending of a Messenger, Prophet Muhammad, from among themselves. Significantly, this verse echoes the very words of Prophet Abraham’s prayer (Verse 129), establishing a direct and meaningful link between the two prophets and their shared message. This affirms that Islam was not a newly invented faith or a response to circumstance, but rather has its roots firmly established in history. Similarly, the direction of prayer being established was not chosen randomly, but as a direct response to Abraham’s passionate and sincere prayers. The mission of Prophet Muhammad involves several crucial roles—reciting Allah's verses (the Qur'an), purifying them from wrong beliefs and conduct, teaching them the Scripture and wisdom, and instructing them in knowledge they did not previously possess. This highlights the transformative impact of Prophet Muhammad's mission on the early Muslim community, elevating them from ignorance and misguided practices to a state of knowledge, purity, and wisdom, preparing them for the important role they are meant to play.

Verse 152

فَاذْكُرُونِي أَذْكُرْكُمْ وَاشْكُرُوا لِي وَلَا تَكْفُرُونِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the discussion of Allah's favours, such as sending a Messenger (Prophet Muhammad) and guiding the believers, this verse invites believers into an intimate, living connection with their Creator through both remembrance and gratitude. Remembering Allah is not merely a ritual but an act that uplifts, purifies, and anchors the believer, bringing tranquility and inviting His special mercy. Expressing gratitude deepens this bond, reminding the believer to acknowledge Allah’s endless favors through obedience, humility, and service. At the same time, the verse carries a subtle yet serious warning: neglecting gratitude and remembrance can lead to spiritual blindness, estrangement, and even denial of the One who bestows all blessings. In a world of distractions and competing narratives, this balance of remembrance and gratitude becomes a shield that preserves the believer’s faith, identity, and connection to Allah—the true source of strength and guidance.

Verse 153

يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا اسْتَعِينُوا بِالصَّبْرِ وَالصَّلَاةِ ۚ إِنَّ اللَّهَ مَعَ الصَّابِرِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse speaks directly to believers, encouraging them to seek strength and support through two important means: patience and prayer. We are reminded that patience and prayer are essential tools, especially when facing the challenges and difficulties inherent in life and in striving to live according to divine guidance. These two qualities are highlighted as primary ways to find help, because Allah is with those who are patient. This is a source of great reassurance, indicating divine support for those who endure patiently. This verse follows broader instructions about the Muslim community's identity and role, providing practical guidance on how to uphold that role through personal discipline and connection with Allah.

Verse 154

وَلَا تَقُولُوا لِمَن يُقْتَلُ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ أَمْوَاتٌ ۚ بَلْ أَحْيَاءٌ وَلَـٰكِن لَّا تَشْعُرُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses a specific, difficult aspect of striving for Allah's cause: the sacrifice of lives. It instructs us not to consider those who are killed while striving in Allah's way as merely "dead." It states clearly that they are, in fact, alive, even if this life is not something we can perceive in our ordinary understanding. This challenges the conventional view of death and emphasizes a deeper reality related to sacrifice for a higher purpose. It connects to the previous verse about patience and prayer by acknowledging that the path requires significant sacrifice, and it offers a profound perspective on the status of those who make the ultimate sacrifice, highlighting their continued existence in a way beyond our immediate perception.

Verse 155

وَلَنَبْلُوَنَّكُم بِشَيْءٍ مِّنَ الْخَوْفِ وَالْجُوعِ وَنَقْصٍ مِّنَ الْأَمْوَالِ وَالْأَنفُسِ وَالثَّمَرَاتِ ۗ وَبَشِّرِ الصَّابِرِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Building on the themes of patience (from Verse 153) and the potential loss of life (from Verse 154), this verse explicitly states that believers will inevitably face trials. These tests may include experiencing fear, hunger, and the loss of possessions, loved ones, or the results of one's efforts. These trials are presented not as punishment, but as part of the process of spiritual growth and establishing one's commitment. Despite these hardships, the verse offers good news specifically to those who are patient during these trials, setting the stage for the description of their admirable response in the next verse.

Verse 156

الَّذِينَ إِذَا أَصَابَتْهُم مُّصِيبَةٌ قَالُوا إِنَّا لِلَّهِ وَإِنَّا إِلَيْهِ رَاجِعُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse describes the response of those who are patient in the face of the calamities mentioned in the previous verse. When afflicted, their statement reflects a core understanding of their relationship with Allah: acknowledging that they belong to Allah and that their ultimate return is to Him. This declaration is an expression of complete submission, recognition of Allah's sovereignty over all things, and acceptance of divine decree. It is this profound mindset, born of patience and reliance on Allah, that distinguishes those to whom glad tidings are promised in Verse 155.

Verse 157

أُولَـٰئِكَ عَلَيْهِمْ صَلَوَاتٌ مِّن رَّبِّهِمْ وَرَحْمَةٌ ۖ وَأُولَـٰئِكَ هُمُ الْمُهْتَدُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Concluding this passage, this verse describes the remarkable reward and status for those who demonstrate patience during trials and affirm their belonging and return to Allah, as mentioned in Verses 155 and 156. Such individuals receive blessings and mercy directly from Allah. Furthermore, they are described as the ones who are rightly guided. This verse highlights the immense value of patience and unwavering faith in the face of adversity, showing that these qualities earn divine favor, compassion, and clear guidance, serving as a powerful motivation to endure hardship for Allah's sake. This is presented as a profound return for the patience and sacrifices described in the preceding verses.

Verse 158

إِنَّ الصَّفَا وَالْمَرْوَةَ مِن شَعَائِرِ اللَّهِ ۖ فَمَنْ حَجَّ الْبَيْتَ أَوِ اعْتَمَرَ فَلَا جُنَاحَ عَلَيْهِ أَن يَطَّوَّفَ بِهِمَا ۚ وَمَن تَطَوَّعَ خَيْرًا فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ شَاكِرٌ عَلِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses a specific ritual during the pilgrimage to the Ka'bah in Mecca. Safa and Marwa are two small hills near the Ka'bah, and walking between them is part of the pilgrimage rites. Before Islam, some people had placed idols on these hills, leading some early Muslims to feel hesitant about performing this walk, fearing it might be associated with those older practices. This verse clarifies that Safa and Marwa are in fact symbols established by Allah, not by pagan tradition. Therefore, walking between them during the pilgrimage (`Hajj` or `Umrah`) is a valid and acceptable act of worship. The verse reassures believers that performing this act, and indeed any voluntary good deed, is recognized and appreciated by Allah, Who is fully aware of our intentions and actions. It shows how Islam purified existing practices, restoring their original purpose as acts devoted purely to Allah.

Verse 159

إِنَّ الَّذِينَ يَكْتُمُونَ مَا أَنزَلْنَا مِنَ الْبَيِّنَاتِ وَالْهُدَىٰ مِن بَعْدِ مَا بَيَّنَّاهُ لِلنَّاسِ فِي الْكِتَابِ ۙ أُولَـٰئِكَ يَلْعَنُهُمُ اللَّهُ وَيَلْعَنُهُمُ اللَّاعِنُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the discussion on religious rituals, this verse delivers a strong warning against those who knowingly conceal the clear truths and guidance that Allah has revealed in His scriptures, even after these truths have been clearly explained. Muslims are warned not to fall into this behavior. The verse states that those who deliberately suppress any part of the revealed guidance—despite its clarity in the Book—are cursed by Allah and by all who are entitled to curse. This highlights the seriousness of hiding or distorting divine truth and emphasizes how essential it is in Islam to uphold and share religious knowledge with honesty and integrity.

Verse 160

إِلَّا الَّذِينَ تَابُوا وَأَصْلَحُوا وَبَيَّنُوا فَأُولَـٰئِكَ أَتُوبُ عَلَيْهِمْ ۚ وَأَنَا التَّوَّابُ الرَّحِيمُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse offers an important exception to the severe warning in the previous verse. It makes it clear that the door to repentance is always open for those who have concealed the truth. If they genuinely regret their actions, correct their behavior, and publicly declare the truth they had previously hidden, then Allah will accept their turning back to Him. Allah is described as being ever-ready to accept repentance and being Most Merciful, emphasizing His boundless grace and willingness to forgive those who sincerely seek His forgiveness and strive to do good.

Verse 161

إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا وَمَاتُوا وَهُمْ كُفَّارٌ أُولَـٰئِكَ عَلَيْهِمْ لَعْنَةُ اللَّهِ وَالْمَلَائِكَةِ وَالنَّاسِ أَجْمَعِينَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Building on the previous warnings, this verse turns to those who completely reject faith and persist in their denial until death. It declares that such individuals will be cursed by Allah, the angels, and all of humanity. This powerfully emphasizes the seriousness of knowingly rejecting truth and guidance once it has been made clear and evident.

Verse 162

خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا ۖ لَا يُخَفَّفُ عَنْهُمُ الْعَذَابُ وَلَا هُمْ يُنظَرُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse describes the consequence for those mentioned in the previous verse—those who reject faith and die in that state. It states that they will abide in a condition of severe punishment, and their suffering will neither be eased nor will they be granted any temporary relief. This serves as a stark warning about the eternal consequences of denying faith and turning away from divine guidance.

Verse 163

وَإِلَـٰهُكُمْ إِلَـٰهٌ وَاحِدٌ ۖ لَّا إِلَـٰهَ إِلَّا هُوَ الرَّحْمَـٰنُ الرَّحِيمُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Transitioning from the warnings about rejecting truth and faith, this verse powerfully reaffirms the central concept of Islam: the absolute oneness of Allah. It declares that the deity you should worship is one and unique; there is no other being worthy of worship but Him. He is further described by two key attributes: Most Gracious and Most Merciful.

Verse 164

إِنَّ فِي خَلْقِ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالْأَرْضِ وَاخْتِلَافِ اللَّيْلِ وَالنَّهَارِ وَالْفُلْكِ الَّتِي تَجْرِي فِي الْبَحْرِ بِمَا يَنفَعُ النَّاسَ وَمَا أَنزَلَ اللَّهُ مِنَ السَّمَاءِ مِن مَّاءٍ فَأَحْيَا بِهِ الْأَرْضَ بَعْدَ مَوْتِهَا وَبَثَّ فِيهَا مِن كُلِّ دَابَّةٍ وَتَصْرِيفِ الرِّيَاحِ وَالسَّحَابِ الْمُسَخَّرِ بَيْنَ السَّمَاءِ وَالْأَرْضِ لَآيَاتٍ لِّقَوْمٍ يَعْقِلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the declaration of Allah's oneness, this verse invites reflection on the natural world as clear evidence of His existence, power, and wisdom. It points to various phenomena: the vastness and intricate design of the heavens and earth, the regular transition between night and day, the ability of ships to navigate the seas for human benefit, the rain that revives dry land and supports diverse life, the multitude of animals spread across the earth, and the movement of winds and clouds. The verse highlights that in all these aspects of creation, there are indeed profound signs and lessons for those who use their intellect and reflect deeply. It suggests that contemplating the wonders of the universe leads to recognizing the one Creator and Sustainer.

Verse 165

وَمِنَ النَّاسِ مَن يَتَّخِذُ مِن دُونِ اللَّهِ أَندَادًا يُحِبُّونَهُمْ كَحُبِّ اللَّهِ ۖ وَالَّذِينَ آمَنُوا أَشَدُّ حُبًّا لِّلَّهِ ۗ وَلَوْ يَرَى الَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا إِذْ يَرَوْنَ الْعَذَابَ أَنَّ الْقُوَّةَ لِلَّهِ جَمِيعًا وَأَنَّ اللَّهَ شَدِيدُ الْعَذَابِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: In contrast to those who reflect on creation and recognize Allah's oneness, this verse points out that some people still worship others alongside Allah, treating them as if they had equal status or importance. They may even show them the kind of devotion and love that is due only to Allah. This is contrasted sharply with believers, whose love for Allah is paramount and surpasses all other loves. The verse warns the wrongdoers that if they could only foresee the consequences of their actions on the Day of Judgment, they would realize that all power belongs solely to Allah and that He is severe in retribution. This sets the stage for the verses that follow, which depict a scene from that Day.

Verse 166

إِذْ تَبَرَّأَ الَّذِينَ اتُّبِعُوا مِنَ الَّذِينَ اتَّبَعُوا وَرَأَوُا الْعَذَابَ وَتَقَطَّعَتْ بِهِمُ الْأَسْبَابُ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Continuing the scene from the Day of Judgment mentioned in the previous verse, this verse describes how those who were followed or worshipped will disassociate themselves from their followers. On that Day, both the followers and those they followed will witness the punishment awaiting them, and any connections, alliances, or relationships they had built on misguided worship will be severed and rendered meaningless. This emphasizes the futility of false allegiances in the face of Allah's ultimate justice.

Verse 167

وَقَالَ الَّذِينَ اتَّبَعُوا لَوْ أَنَّ لَنَا كَرَّةً فَنَتَبَرَّأَ مِنْهُمْ كَمَا تَبَرَّءُوا مِنَّا ۗ كَذَٰلِكَ يُرِيهِمُ اللَّهُ أَعْمَالَهُمْ حَسَرَاتٍ عَلَيْهِمْ ۖ وَمَا هُم بِخَارِجِينَ مِنَ النَّارِ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Concluding this scene of the Day of Judgment, this verse captures the bitter regret of the followers. Seeing those they had followed disown them, they will desperately wish for another chance in earthly life so they could renounce those they once followed. The verse states that Allah will show them the consequences of their worldly actions in a way that fills them with intense remorse. It ends by confirming that they will have no escape from the severe punishment—referred to as the Fire. This serves as a powerful reminder of the importance of making the right choices and establishing a true relationship with Allah in this life, before it is too late.

Verse 168

يَا أَيُّهَا النَّاسُ كُلُوا مِمَّا فِي الْأَرْضِ حَلَالًا طَيِّبًا وَلَا تَتَّبِعُوا خُطُوَاتِ الشَّيْطَانِ ۚ إِنَّهُ لَكُمْ عَدُوٌّ مُّبِينٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse addresses all of humanity, granting broad permission to enjoy the good and lawful things available on Earth. It emphasizes that Allah has created these resources for human benefit. The verse calls for rejecting taboos related to food and drink that are based on superstition or irrational cultural practices. Following this permission, the verse warns against following the suggestions of Satan—referred to as "the evil one." It highlights that Satan is a clear and declared enemy to humanity, and his influence must be avoided. This introduces the idea that although many things are permitted, proper guidance is necessary to discern what is truly beneficial and lawful—and that guidance comes from Allah, not from humanity’s enemy.

Verse 169

إِنَّمَا يَأْمُرُكُم بِالسُّوءِ وَالْفَحْشَاءِ وَأَن تَقُولُوا عَلَى اللَّهِ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Building on the previous verse, this highlights the nature of Satan's influence. It explains that Satan prompts people towards actions that are wrong and indecent. Crucially, it also warns that Satan encourages people to speak about Allah without proper knowledge. This includes making declarations about what is permissible or forbidden without divine guidance, linking the issue of lawful food (mentioned in 168) to the source of religious authority. Following Satan means stepping away from truth and proper knowledge regarding Allah's commands.

Verse 170

وَإِذَا قِيلَ لَهُمُ اتَّبِعُوا مَا أَنزَلَ اللَّهُ قَالُوا بَلْ نَتَّبِعُ مَا أَلْفَيْنَا عَلَيْهِ آبَاءَنَا ۗ أَوَلَوْ كَانَ آبَاؤُهُمْ لَا يَعْقِلُونَ شَيْئًا وَلَا يَهْتَدُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse presents a common human response when confronted with divine revelation: a resistance to change and a preference for tradition over guidance. It describes people who, when asked to follow what Allah has revealed (which includes guidance on what is lawful and right), choose instead to stick to the practices and beliefs they inherited from their ancestors. The verse then critically questions this attitude, asking if they would still follow their forefathers' ways even if those ways lacked wisdom and true guidance. This highlights the importance of using one's reason and seeking true guidance from Allah, rather than blindly adhering to inherited customs, especially in matters of faith and law. This connects directly to the previous verses by showing how Satan's influence can lead to rejecting revealed truth in favor of misguided traditions.

Verse 171

وَمَثَلُ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا كَمَثَلِ الَّذِي يَنْعِقُ بِمَا لَا يَسْمَعُ إِلَّا دُعَاءً وَنِدَاءً ۚ صُمٌّ بُكْمٌ عُمْيٌ فَهُمْ لَا يَعْقِلُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse uses a powerful comparison to describe the state of those who reject faith and guidance, particularly those who cling blindly to inherited ways as mentioned in the previous verse. It likens them to animals (specifically sheep or goats) who hear only the sounds and shouts of a shepherd but do not understand the meaning or instruction behind the calls. The verse then describes them as "deaf, dumb, and blind," not necessarily physically, but in terms of their inability or unwillingness to truly listen to, understand, or see the truth of divine guidance. It concludes that because of this state, they lack true wisdom or understanding of spiritual matters.

Verse 172

يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا كُلُوا مِن طَيِّبَاتِ مَا رَزَقْنَاكُمْ وَاشْكُرُوا لِلَّهِ إِن كُنتُمْ إِيَّاهُ تَعْبُدُونَ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Shifting focus from the general address to humanity (as in verse 168) and the condition of the disbelievers (verse 171), this verse speaks specifically to the believers. It reiterates the permission to eat the "good things" that Allah has provided, but adds that believers must be grateful to Allah. This links the enjoyment of lawful provisions directly to the act of worship. It implies that recognizing Allah as the provider—and expressing gratitude for His blessings—is a fundamental part of truly worshipping Him alone. This verse also sets the stage for the more detailed regulations about forbidden foods that follow.

Verse 173

إِنَّمَا حَرَّمَ عَلَيْكُمُ الْمَيْتَةَ وَالدَّمَ وَلَحْمَ الْخِنزِيرِ وَمَا أُهِلَّ بِهِ لِغَيْرِ اللَّهِ ۖ فَمَنِ اضْطُرَّ غَيْرَ بَاغٍ وَلَا عَادٍ فَلَا إِثْمَ عَلَيْهِ ۚ إِنَّ اللَّهَ غَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: Following the general permission to eat lawful things and the instruction to be grateful (verse 172), this verse provides specific details about what is forbidden for believers. It lists four main categories: dead meat, blood, the flesh of swine (pigs), and meat from animals on which a name other than Allah's was invoked at the time of slaughter (For a more comprehensive enumeration of the forbidden kinds of flesh, see Sura 5:3). The verse also includes an important exception: if someone is facing necessity and has no other choice, consuming something from this list is permissible, provided it is not done out of willful disobedience or exceeding the needed amount. This highlights Allah's mercy and forgiveness, showing that the law considers difficult circumstances. The prohibition of meat sacrificed to others reinforces the central theme of worshipping Allah alone, even in the act of eating.

Verse 174

إِنَّ الَّذِينَ يَكْتُمُونَ مَا أَنزَلَ اللَّهُ مِنَ الْكِتَابِ وَيَشْتَرُونَ بِهِ ثَمَنًا قَلِيلًا ۙ أُولَـٰئِكَ مَا يَأْكُلُونَ فِي بُطُونِهِمْ إِلَّا النَّارَ وَلَا يُكَلِّمُهُمُ اللَّهُ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ وَلَا يُزَكِّيهِمْ وَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ
Translation: not yet
Reflection: This verse shifts the focus once again, moving from dietary laws to a strong condemnation of those who conceal or distort the truth revealed by Allah in the divine scriptures.
